# AI Media Scout — 2026-11-25

The edition has 84 complete guides and 7 screened discoveries. It completes 20 earlier pending guides and details 5 substantive release updates. Velorn v0.4.3 supplies reference imagery for every named cast member in a shot. Phosphene documents a local named-voice route for two authorized characters. Local photo culling, animation inbetweens and editing from recorded dialogue are covered alongside less common studio workflows. OpenSloper exposes editable costume patterns to a model client, dScribe drafts alt text for human review, and Sound2Hap explores turning short environmental sounds into tactile cues. Code grants were read in full. Models, services, creative inputs and hardware or software hosts have separate requirements. This edition is based on source review and published documentation. No discovered app was installed or run. The expanded plan covered 56 fields, 345/345 repository-query attempts and 21 model tasks. The repository lane recorded 345 successful, 0 partial and 0 failed queries, with 99 bounded. The model lane recorded 0 failed tasks. Metadata from 480 saved repository pages included 19,283 numeric identities. The complete retained backlog spans 124,309 candidates, including pilot history and 1,075 previously screened guides still pending before this edition. Creative relevance and the complete software grant were checked before including any discovery. Unreviewed and excluded records stay in local research. Live primary research yielded 217 complete usable responses from 252 distinct URL attempts, including a non-GitHub source in each of 56 fields. 0 captures reached the size bound and 35 other sources were inaccessible or unusable. The ecosystem checks cover all 8 configured groups and record available findings and gaps. Evidence labels in this edition are 0 Recommended, 0 Ready to try, 85 Quality unverified, 6 Experimental. Independent use remains unverified for 88 entries. The creative result check remains unverified for 90, and model, service or cost requirements remain unverified for 91. Repository searches returned no saved rows for Embodied and tactile interactive art, Multiband art and heritage imaging. Primary web research for these fields is included in the coverage notes. 21 model tasks reached the configured response limit of 50 rows. A model record remains a discovery lead until code and use are verified. The separate watchlist made 68 unsuccessful repository checks. Collection logged 8 enrichment failures. Credential-shaped material in unsealed captures was removed before sealing. The affected source origins and before-and-after hashes remain in private evidence.

Documentation reviewed today. Tools are not hands-on tested unless explicitly stated. Requirements and performance remain source-specific.

| Images & design | Invoke documents image-model workflows served through an editable studio. Split image-to-image inference from the layer or canvas editing interface. Compare generated inbetweens over a hard cut and retain the original timing. ## Search and review counts

56 creative fields; 345/345 repository queries attempted; 21/21 model-task queries attempted. 21576 distinct source candidates and 981 model leads. 84 detailed profiles and 7 additional screened discoveries. Source gaps: 0 failed repository queries, 0 partial repository queries, 99 bounded repository queries, 0 failed model-task queries and 0 web ecosystem gaps. Raw search candidates include duplicates of known tools, excluded projects and projects awaiting review; they are not verified recommendations.

## Research beyond GitHub

- **gitlab** · searched: Seven project-search terms returned one page each, with six reaching the 20-row request bound. The complete Skiagrafia software grant and learned segmentation-to-vector workflow were checked directly. Other candidates remained private when the code grant or practical AI scope failed review. [Source](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/81371091/repository/files/README.md/raw?ref=HEAD) [Source](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/81371091/repository/files/LICENSE/raw?ref=HEAD)
- **codeberg** · searched: Five generic API searches returned HTTP 429, limiting breadth. Direct access provided the full software grant, source overview and release record for Facetracker, a Linux OpenSeeFace wrapper. An already-profiled Whisper talk-subtitle project was also accessible by direct source. Release and software notices were independently checked before inclusion. [Source](https://formai.studio/formdam/) [Source](https://openscene.app/) [Source](https://www.tensorstack.ai/amuse.html)
- **research-code** · searched: The Ambisonizer paper and released implementation describe neural B-format audio synthesis. A second scene-sound paper had demos and a project page, without a released implementation established in this review. [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.13428) [Source](https://masaki-lmd.github.io/scene2sound/) [Source](https://huggingface.co/OpenMOSS-Team/MOVA-720p)
- **international** · searched: Chinese publisher documentation shows local screen-caption inference. Japanese and Chinese workflow sources include editable text, stage-specific models and release-specific exports. The Portuguese workflow describes separate editable timeline and captions. First complete guide in the library. The publication and documentation dates are not necessarily the project’s launch date.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Creative use and the code grant are documented. Check-level gaps remain before a recommendation.

Scope: Caption a short review cut on an iPhone or iPad, then correct names and export captions or a rendered copy.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-25. No local execution implied.

A selected speech model creates word-level timings. The editor lets you correct individual words, retime them and apply styling before exporting. ### First project

A small first project makes the input, model output and handoff easy to inspect. [Source 1](https://github.com/leogaudin/opencaptions/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/leogaudin/opencaptions/releases/tag/v1.3.4) [Source 3](https://docs.opencaptions.app/guides/iphone-app/) [Source 4](https://apps.apple.com/app/id6820548922) [Source 5](https://docs.opencaptions.app/)

1. Listen through a first pass and fix names or incorrectly split words.
2. Check the caption layout and retime against the intended cut.
3. Export a review copy or a subtitle file with the selected route.

## OpenCaptions (transcript-based editor)

The source repository was created on 2026-10-28. This is its first complete guide in the library.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Creative use and the code grant are documented. Check-level gaps remain before a recommendation.

Scope: Assemble a talking-head first cut from selected transcript sentences and check captions on the result.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-25. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** Reviewed the complete project software grant and its redistribution conditions. Model, data and service terms are scoped separately. [Source](https://github.com/AmirB22/OpenCaptions/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The command-line and local UI examples describe sentence selection, caption styling and cutting near quiet points and hard picture cuts. [Source](https://github.com/AmirB22/OpenCaptions/blob/main/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** The installation and first-use steps are in the cited documentation. Reproduction of this workflow remains unverified. [Source](https://github.com/AmirB22/OpenCaptions/blob/main/README.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** The reviewed sources do not establish workflow-specific support, fixes and unresolved blockers.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use of the exact workflow was not established in this source review.

The scoped source workflow lists no mandatory paid speech provider. [Source](https://github.com/AmirB22/OpenCaptions/blob/main/README.md)

Source and documentation review. No hands-on execution by AI Media Scout. Compare the automatic cuts against the original clip. Export continuity is source-reviewed only.

### How it uses AI

Parakeet or Whisper generates word-timed text. Users select sentences and the editor aligns export cuts with silence and picture boundaries. [Source](https://github.com/AmirB22/OpenCaptions/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

Parakeet or Whisper generates word-timed text. Users select sentences and the editor aligns export cuts with silence and picture boundaries. [Source 1](https://github.com/AmirB22/OpenCaptions/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Assemble a talking-head first cut from selected transcript sentences and check captions on the result. [Source 1](https://github.com/AmirB22/OpenCaptions/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The command-line and local UI examples describe sentence selection, caption styling and cutting near quiet points and hard picture cuts. [Source 1](https://github.com/AmirB22/OpenCaptions/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Follow the documented route for the scoped workflow. The steps below are source references. [Source 1](https://github.com/AmirB22/OpenCaptions/blob/main/README.md)

1. Use the documented Linux or Windows Python and FFmpeg setup.
2. Fetch the language-appropriate model and fonts and keep job data on local storage.
### First project

A small first project makes the input, model output and handoff easy to inspect. [Source 1](https://github.com/AmirB22/OpenCaptions/blob/main/README.md)

1. Analyze a sample clip and review its words and timings.
2. Select a few sentences and export a captioned take.
3. Review both sides of each join for lost words, stray frames and timing errors.
### Hardware & software

Platform and compute constraints refer to the selected route. [Source 1](https://github.com/AmirB22/OpenCaptions/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** The README estimates 0.5 GB for the English model and 1.5 GB for Whisper Medium, plus the environment and space for working copies.
- **Software:** Python and FFmpeg. The documented English route uses Parakeet. Other languages use Whisper through faster-whisper.
- **Platforms:** Linux and Windows installation is documented. macOS availability and accelerator minima are undocumented.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Dependencies and creative assets carry their own terms. [Source 1](https://github.com/AmirB22/OpenCaptions/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/AmirB22/OpenCaptions/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT app. Speech models and fonts carry separate terms. Captions and cuts derive from the source recording.
- **Commercial:** Project code is governed by MIT. Confirm selected model, source-media and output restrictions separately.
- **Cost:** Local compute and downloaded model storage. The scoped source workflow lists no mandatory paid speech provider.

### Why it merits attention

The creative workflow is grounded in the cited documentation. Output quality and compatibility are not hands-on verified. [Source 1](https://github.com/AmirB22/OpenCaptions/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Compare the automatic cuts against the original clip. Export continuity is source-reviewed only. [Source 1](https://github.com/AmirB22/OpenCaptions/blob/main/README.md)

1. Import a small image sequence and group it in display order.

A trained convolutional model translates short environmental sounds into vibrations. The accompanying study compares sound and vibration pairs with fixed signal-processing approaches. - **Maintenance and support · unknown:** The reviewed sources do not establish workflow-specific support, fixes and unresolved blockers.
- **Independent use · verified:** Lippmann and coauthors report using the exact Manga OCR project for Japanese text extraction before translation and lettering. The pipeline illustrates text and panel detection and OCR output in Figure 5. [Source](https://aclanthology.org/2025.coling-main.232.pdf)
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** Apache-2.0 code. The base model and artwork rights are separate, especially for redistribution. Local compute and model storage. No mandatory model service was documented. [Source](https://github.com/kha-white/manga-ocr/blob/master/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/kha-white/manga-ocr/releases/tag/v0.1.16) [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/manga-ocr/json) [Source](https://huggingface.co/kha-white/manga-ocr-base)

Source and documentation review. No hands-on execution by AI Media Scout. A crop at a time is not full-page reading-order inference. Be careful with punctuation, decorative text and mixed orientation. The independent experiment demonstrates integration in a printed-panel workflow. Its translation evaluation is system-level and does not isolate the accuracy of the current package.

### How it uses AI

A vision-to-text model reads cropped Japanese manga text, including panel typography and varied orientations. ### How it uses AI

Local Depth Anything V2 inference estimates a flat source image’s distance map. The app warps it into a stereo pair for a headset, 3D TV or anaglyph preview. [Source 1](https://github.com/zimba7768/cineply-cut/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/zimba7768/cineply-cut/releases/tag/v0.1.1)

### What it is good for

Build a short edit using source footage with one clearly marked generated insert. [Source 1](https://github.com/zimba7768/cineply-cut/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/zimba7768/cineply-cut/releases/tag/v0.1.1)

### Demo & examples

The README documents the media bin and per-generation cost estimate alongside crossfades and editable titles. [Source 1](https://github.com/zimba7768/cineply-cut/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/zimba7768/cineply-cut/releases/tag/v0.1.1)

### Install

Follow the documented route for the scoped workflow. The steps below are source references. [Source 1](https://github.com/zimba7768/cineply-cut/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/zimba7768/cineply-cut/releases/tag/v0.1.1)

1. Use the documented Windows release installer from the official project.
2. Prepare a review project. Connect a Higgsfield account only if using hosted generation.
### First project

A small first project makes the input, model output and handoff easy to inspect. [Source 1](https://github.com/zimba7768/cineply-cut/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/zimba7768/cineply-cut/releases/tag/v0.1.1)

1. Import two media clips and make a local edit.
2. For a generated insert, review what the service receives and its stated cost, then inspect the result in the project before export.
### Hardware & software

Platform and compute constraints refer to the selected route. [Source 1](https://github.com/zimba7768/cineply-cut/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/zimba7768/cineply-cut/releases/tag/v0.1.1)

- **Hardware:** Minimum RAM, VRAM and disk space for the scoped workflow are undocumented.
- **Software:** Windows desktop editor. Optional hosted model generation and built-in Windows speech use separate routes.
- **Platforms:** Windows is the documented application platform. macOS/Linux distributions were not documented.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Dependencies and creative assets carry their own terms. [Source 1](https://github.com/zimba7768/cineply-cut/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/zimba7768/cineply-cut/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/zimba7768/cineply-cut/releases/tag/v0.1.1)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT editor. Higgsfield service and models retain their own terms.
- **Commercial:** Project code is governed by MIT. Confirm selected model, source-media and output restrictions separately.
- **Cost:** Local editing is free per the source. AI generations are billed to the chosen provider balance.

### Why it merits attention

The creative workflow is grounded in the cited documentation. Output quality and compatibility are not hands-on verified. [Source 1](https://github.com/zimba7768/cineply-cut/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/zimba7768/cineply-cut/releases/tag/v0.1.1)

### Limitations

Installer signing and provider availability are separate constraints. Retain independent copies of original project media. [Source 1](https://github.com/zimba7768/cineply-cut/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/zimba7768/cineply-cut/releases/tag/v0.1.1)

Source and documentation review. No hands-on execution by AI Media Scout. The published timing is a single maintainer observation. No independent tracker-to-avatar test or generic live-performance latency has been verified for this wrapper.

### How it uses AI

A Linux desktop wrapper configures and runs OpenSeeFace facial landmark inference on webcam frames. It sends pose and expression data to the selected avatar application. [Source](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/ZRayEntertainment/Facetracker/raw/README.md?ref=main) [Source](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/ZRayEntertainment/Facetracker/releases/tags/26.8.1) [Source](https://docs.z-ray.de/en/facetracker/) [Source](https://flathub.org/en/apps/de.z_ray.Facetracker) [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/flathub/de.z_ray.Facetracker/master/de.z_ray.Facetracker.json) [Source](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/ZRayEntertainment/Facetracker/releases?limit=4)

### Introduction

A Linux desktop wrapper configures and runs OpenSeeFace facial landmark inference on webcam frames. It sends pose and expression data to the selected avatar application. [Source 1](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/ZRayEntertainment/Facetracker/raw/README.md?ref=main) [Source 2](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/ZRayEntertainment/Facetracker/releases/tags/26.8.1) [Source 3](https://docs.z-ray.de/en/facetracker/) [Source 4](https://flathub.org/en/apps/de.z_ray.Facetracker) [Source 5](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/flathub/de.z_ray.Facetracker/master/de.z_ray.Facetracker.json) [Source 6](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/ZRayEntertainment/Facetracker/releases?limit=4)

### What it is good for

Drive a virtual performer with a Linux webcam while keeping the tracker and avatar renderer as separate components. [Source 1](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/ZRayEntertainment/Facetracker/raw/README.md?ref=main) [Source 2](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/ZRayEntertainment/Facetracker/releases/tags/26.8.1) [Source 3](https://docs.z-ray.de/en/facetracker/) [Source 4](https://flathub.org/en/apps/de.z_ray.Facetracker) [Source 5](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/flathub/de.z_ray.Facetracker/master/de.z_ray.Facetracker.json) [Source 6](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/ZRayEntertainment/Facetracker/releases?limit=4)

### Demo & examples

The publisher documents a webcam-to-avatar workflow and records using a Raspberry Pi 400 at 640×360 with roughly seven tracking frames per second. That is one test configuration. [Source 1](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/ZRayEntertainment/Facetracker/raw/README.md?ref=main) [Source 2](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/ZRayEntertainment/Facetracker/releases/tags/26.8.1) [Source 3](https://docs.z-ray.de/en/facetracker/) [Source 4](https://flathub.org/en/apps/de.z_ray.Facetracker) [Source 5](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/flathub/de.z_ray.Facetracker/master/de.z_ray.Facetracker.json) [Source 6](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/ZRayEntertainment/Facetracker/releases?limit=4)

### Install

Follow the documented route for the scoped workflow. The steps below are source references. [Source 1](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/ZRayEntertainment/Facetracker/raw/README.md?ref=main) [Source 2](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/ZRayEntertainment/Facetracker/releases/tags/26.8.1) [Source 3](https://docs.z-ray.de/en/facetracker/) [Source 4](https://flathub.org/en/apps/de.z_ray.Facetracker) [Source 5](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/flathub/de.z_ray.Facetracker/master/de.z_ray.Facetracker.json) [Source 6](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/ZRayEntertainment/Facetracker/releases?limit=4)

1. Open the Facetracker package listing and follow its Flatpak installation route for your Linux architecture.
2. Open Facetracker, select the webcam and set the receiving address and port to match your avatar renderer.
### First project

A small first project makes the input, model output and handoff easy to inspect. [Source 1](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/ZRayEntertainment/Facetracker/raw/README.md?ref=main) [Source 2](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/ZRayEntertainment/Facetracker/releases/tags/26.8.1) [Source 3](https://docs.z-ray.de/en/facetracker/) [Source 4](https://flathub.org/en/apps/de.z_ray.Facetracker) [Source 5](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/flathub/de.z_ray.Facetracker/master/de.z_ray.Facetracker.json) [Source 6](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/ZRayEntertainment/Facetracker/releases?limit=4)

1. Start a small on-screen rehearsal and check the selected tracking model.
2. Compare neutral, head-turn and expressive poses with the visible avatar.
3. Confirm tracking cadence and recovery after turning away before using it for a take.
### Hardware & software

Platform and compute constraints refer to the selected route. [Source 1](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/ZRayEntertainment/Facetracker/raw/README.md?ref=main) [Source 2](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/ZRayEntertainment/Facetracker/releases/tags/26.8.1) [Source 3](https://docs.z-ray.de/en/facetracker/) [Source 4](https://flathub.org/en/apps/de.z_ray.Facetracker) [Source 5](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/flathub/de.z_ray.Facetracker/master/de.z_ray.Facetracker.json) [Source 6](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/ZRayEntertainment/Facetracker/releases?limit=4)

- **Hardware:** The maintainers list tested webcams and one Raspberry Pi 400 example. The package listing reports a 175 MiB download. Minimum RAM, VRAM and sustained rehearsal frame rate are undocumented.
- **Software:** Linux with a webcam and the packaged GTK/libadwaita interface. The Flatpak manifest builds OpenSeeFace and includes ONNX Runtime. A separate avatar receiver renders the animation.
- **Platforms:** The publisher and Flathub document Linux x86_64 and aarch64. The renderer can have a different OS and its own runtime requirements.

- **Commercial:** Project code is governed by MIT. Confirm selected model, source-media and output restrictions separately.
- **Cost:** The Flatpak is free. Local compute, a webcam and a renderer are needed. A selected renderer or character may have its own costs.

### Genchanted · MIT

Create a rough prop directly in an XR prototype and inspect it in the scene before retopology.
A model-backed generator creates a mesh from text. A Godot XR project streams generated vertices and places the resulting object in a running scene.
